In this regard, we intend to leave a statement to the record, re-launching the call made on Saturday by the order of doctors: We call on Parliament to respect science. ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u218	BRIZIERELLI (L-SP-PSd'Az). Mr President, Amendment 8.0.2 concerns MOCAs, i.e. materials and articles that come into contact with food. It was proposed by the League, but it was signed by senators of all parliamentary groups, with the exception of the PD which, we have learned, has tabled a similar amendment. The amendment was requested by the Municipality of Deruta, also on behalf of other administrations registered with the National Association of Ceramic City, during an official meeting with the Undersecretary Lucia Borgonzoni. Specifically, it provides for the reopening for four months of the terms referred to in Article 6 of Legislative Decree No. 29 of 2017 on the sanctioning rules for the violation of Community regulations in the matter of MOCA. Article 6 provided for the communication to the ASLs responsible for the territory of the establishments concerned and provided for a penalty of between EUR 1,500 and EUR 9,000 for non-communication. With this intervention we allow the companies to be able to adjust and to regularize their position. Of course, the need to make Italian productions understand and evaluate in Europe the peculiarities and uniqueness of Italian productions remains open. In this case, allow me, with all due respect, to highlight the difference between a handmade ceramic dish and a slightly sad white bowl produced at an industrial level in some other European country. So our vote will obviously be in favour. [[Applause from the L-SP-PSd'Az Group.]]

The main assumption is as follows: the times of the emergency and the reconstruction are also slipping ope legis, in the sense that the law has recognized the persistence of the critical and emergency conditions that justify this state due to the reconstruction; however, the measures launched have not been extended at the time when, perhaps with a little 'optimism, it was thought that today the emergency would be overcome for which those actions had been thought in constancy of this situation. It is as if the state were saying something like this: I recognise that we are late, so the emergency will last another year or two (in some cases the duration is expected until 2020). However, there are questions relating to the provision of recruitment in the municipalities through collaboration contracts, to hire and maintain on the spot staff now qualified that would not be found in the normal rankings; on the urban free zone, to allow businesses to go to those territories and obtain economic and social tax breaks in order to be able to settle, as well as on the coverage of the greater burdens due to the municipalities. I only set the example of the Eagle, but it would be worth it for any other municipality. At L'Aquila this coverage is particularly expensive because we are talking about a municipality whose entire historical centre is still depopulated and therefore must organize the services (public transport, waste collection) on a much larger territorial extension; therefore, while this municipality has less revenue because the houses, which do not live because they are still dangerous, do not produce IMU revenues, neither in compensation nor directly (if they were second homes), the Municipality spends more money to bring the bus, the collection of waste, the school buses for children and every other service in the fractions now more and more distant. This determines for that Municipality (but I think similar examples can be made for several others even if smaller) the impossibility of closing the budget. All the past years the Municipality of L'Aquila has had interlocutions with the Government uniquely, but today we do not even know who answers for the problems of the seismic crater; the previous Government has chosen the commissioner and therefore there was a commissioner who dealt with it. Do you want to name another one or do you think you can confirm this? Do we want to overcome (as we think is best) the regime of the police station? Let there be a Minister, a mission structure, a Undersecretary, a manager with whom the mayors of the crater can speak instead of speaking with ten different ministers for each of the individual competences. We call for these amendments to be adopted. ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u235	Mr President, I would like to express my support for all the amendments tabled by the Free and Equal Group to Article 9, but I would like to speak on a human case that has affected the whole country, namely that of Grandma Peppina, the 95-year-old Giuseppina Fattori, who saw her house in abusive wood seized, where she would like to live without leaving her land, after she was shocked by the earthquake. The case relates to amendment 9.0.0,100, proposed by the Government and accepted by the Commission, which makes a series of amendments to the discipline contained in Article 8-a of Decree-Law No 189 of 17 October 2016, as recently rewritten by Article 07 of Decree-Law No 55 of 2018. This amendment aims to remedy the critical issues highlighted by the President of the Republic following the promulgation of the conversion law proper to Decree-Law No 55 of 2018 in a direct letter to the President of the Count Council. I would ask the rapporteur and the Government, if they can, to pay attention. The President of the Republic, inter alia, wrote in this letter: I cannot help but point out some of the critical aspects of Article 07 which, while not clearly violating constitutional legitimacy, raise serious concerns. Among other things, President Mattarella continued - the paragraph 3 provides for the ineffectiveness - as well as administrative measures - also of the preventive seizure. The provision is unsystematic and detrimental to the intangibility of judicial measures, subtracting the judiciary's exclusive competence to assess the conditions for the continuation of seizure measures. With this amendment, expunging the reference to the preventive seizure, it is limited, according to the suggestion of the Quirinale, the ineffectiveness previewed from the norm to the orders of demolition and return in pristino, that is administrative measures, and no more reference is made to the measures of preemptive seizure. In addition, with the addition of a new period, it is expected that: For works and works that comply with the conditions referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article, the provisions of both documentary and preventive sequestration, adopted until the date of July 25, 2018, for the violation of the building and landscape regulations, shall be revoked. In a nutshell, this discipline is aimed at sheltering, in the blackberries of the reconstruction process, the victim of the earthquake that carried out temporary abusive installations from possible criminal sanctions and any orders for demolition, restoration in pristina or seizure measures. I would like it to be clear, however, that such a discipline, defined as the rule saves Grandma Peppina, would once again risk not producing the desired effect, namely to return Grandma Peppina to her wooden house, as among the conditions introduced by Article 07 of Decree-Law No 55 of 2018 there is that the aforementioned works or artifacts or structures consist of installations of light works, even prefabricated, and structures of any kind ( caravans, campers, mobile homes) that are used as houses that are however removable, that must be able to move. [[Call the president back.]] Allow me, please: we have time and we can use it. Such can not be considered the construction realized for grandmother Peppina, solidly fixed to the ground on a cement base, at least according to the attorney of Macerata, that however, with an interpretation that relates to the original norm of Decree-Law 189 of 2016 rather than to the recent modification entered into force on 25 July last, deposited, on 4 August last, with the gip, the opinion favorable to the dissectro of the structure and now expects the decision of the gip. But, as you can see, the magistrate's careful and prudent assessment is needed.

ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u355	Mr President, I would like to explain Amendment No 11.7. Article 37- bis, paragraph 1, of Legislative Decree no. 385 of September 1, 1993, provides for the possibility to establish, in the autonomous Provinces of Trento and Bolzano, provincial cooperative banking groups. In this way the legislator has taken into account the particularity of the system of cooperative credit banks present in the two Provinces, especially in South Tyrol, where the provincial banking group between the Raiffeisen banks and the rural ones is currently being formed. The Raiffeisen coffers are closely linked to the territory and have helped to effectively support the development of the local economy, mainly made up of small and medium-sized enterprises. In view of the fact that Article 5 of the Decree of the President of the Republic of 31 August 1972, No 670 (which is the Statute of autonomy of the Trentino-Alto Adige Region) confers on the Region concurrent legislative competence in the field of the ordering of rural coffers and regional credit companies, we propose that the President of the Trentino-Alto Adige/Südtirol Region be given the competence to establish, for its provincial group, the minimum threshold of participation of rural coffers and Raiffeisen coffers in the capital of its parent. That is the content of our amendment and, since it concerns our territory, we are calling for it to be approved by this House.

STEGER (Aut (SVP-PATT, UV)). Mr President, our Group has merely tabled targeted amendments, simplification for businesses, the agricultural sector and craftsmen. I'll quote three. The amendment aimed at extending the deadline for the new requirements of hotel facilities and Alpine shelters on fire safety by six months. The aim was not to force hoteliers to carry out the work in the middle of the tourist season, that is the summer of next year. The amendment, which is important for the agricultural sector, was intended to extend the entry into force of the rule on the traceability of payments. The aim was to give time to ABI and Poste Italiane to put in place initiatives aimed at reducing the bureaucratic impact. Also for agricultural companies, the extension on the mandatory anti-mafia certification for the purpose of applying for European contributions. (At 14.12 a.m.) (Follow Steger). The motivation was to avoid the real risk that the offices in charge would not be able to examine all the practices, with relative loss of contributions. Unfortunately, the government did not accept these and other proposals of pure common sense, limiting itself to accepting only the one on the adaptation of alpine shelters. At least Alpine shelters now have time to comply with fire regulations. On the other hand, we welcome the decision to extend the time limits for group membership. We had tabled an amendment on the Raiffeisen to be the President of the Region, after consulting the Bank of Italy, to establish the thresholds for the participation of cooperative credit banks in the parent company. We believe that it would have been useful and appropriate, because the Presidency of the Region has full knowledge of the territory. As this is a grouping of banks at South Tyrol's territorial level, we would have seen well this rule which, unfortunately, the Government and the majority did not grant. This is something to which we must return in the future. We need an in-depth reflection as regards cooperative credit in order to safeguard the mutual principles and the link with the territory, which are the founding values of cooperative credit. The biggest limit of the reform is the obligation to join a cooperative banking group, or the loss of a banking licence. Solidarity to ensure the stability of the system cannot compromise its identity and freedom of economic initiative, which is, moreover, guaranteed by the Italian Constitution. We therefore call for the time that has been gained with the extension to be used to resolve all the problems involved in the reform and this reflection, and I call on the majority of this Parliament. We are not among those who criticize the extension-term instrument as the first real act in this House of the new Government. We believe it is legitimate, provided that this time is used in a profitable way for cooperative credit and for all other areas affected by the measure. In any case, it strikes us that there has been no real willingness to confront various proposals, including our own, which did not require any economic commitment and which raised real issues. A little more good will and common sense would have sufficed. For these reasons, I am announcing the Group's abstention vote for the Autonomies. [[Applause from the Aut Group (SVP-PATT, UV).]]

I remember that the Minister of the Interior, right here in the Senate, in front of the Constitutional Affairs Commissions of the two Chambers, last July 25 stated that a reform is needed to ensure that this body that exists to continue working for the good of the territories and citizens. It is not true, therefore, that we were not interested in the matter; this was simply not the time to do so. The same applies to local authorities. We have examined the package of amendments relating to the municipalities, but in this case too, except for some commitment from the Government, it was decided to postpone the debate because it was not urgent for August and from here to the end of the year. We'll discuss this later. I would also like to remind you of the important measures adopted by the Decree-Law in question, which primarily concern the areas affected by the earthquake, as well as the indispensable contribution made by the Council of Ministers by extending the state of emergency in Ischia, as it expired precisely in August. But important measures have also been envisaged, such as the one that guarantees children to go to school even in classes where the number of pupils would not be sufficient; extensions have been given for the adaptation of the fire regulations and for the verification of seismic vulnerabilities; and this happens immediately to ensure that children go to school. For the same reason it has been decided not to apply the sanction - I stress the sanction - for those children who have not submitted the vaccination certification. The vaccination obligation has not been abolished, but one of the sanctions has been abolished to ensure that children from zero to six years of age - as well as their companions from six to sixteen years of age - are able to go to school not to discriminate against children, in case their parents have not yet complied with them. This too will be discussed later and, therefore, I find the opposition's criticism very presumptuous, also from this point of view. Another very important measure: we saved the Monza Grand Prix, which, for bureaucratic issues, could no longer take place, resulting in damage not only to the image of the country but also to the economy of entire territories. We helped local television stations, which would risk leaving some 5,000 people at home, because they could not benefit from the contributions granted. Here too, attention has been paid to the territories, specifically to local information, which is fundamental and guarantees attention to our citizens, even with direct links, through commitment on our territories. We have planned a number of interventions with regard to school. For example, we have extended to the academic year 2018/2019 the possibility of resorting to the exhausted ranking for the assignment of teaching assignments in the courses of high artistic, musical and choreutica, and at the same time of sending to the Italian schools abroad the selection of teachers extending the rankings. As far as universities are concerned, we have extended to 31 October the time limits for the committees which are proceeding to confer the national scientific qualification for the roles of university professors. In the field of culture, we have extended - although it is not enough for the opposition - also to 2018 the cultural bonus for eighteen-year-olds. Only measures aimed at the citizens, designed for the citizens, and we have not finished here. We have approved measures aimed at and designed for the citizens, and we have not finished. I wonder how it is possible for the opposition to think that in two months after the government's establishment all the problems that have been caused in recent years can be solved. [[Applause from the L-SP-PSd'Az and M5S Groups]] I also remember the special attention paid to our territories and our regions. For example, we have adopted an amendment in which we have reduced the blockage in the delivery of assignments by the Ministry of Transport for the financing of local public transport. This has a lot to do with everyday life, especially our commuting citizens, but not only, and all the kids who go to school. We have introduced a number of innovations and also helped the people in economic difficulty by suspending the capital share of loans for families in difficulty and we have safeguarded jobs - as I said before - in the territories thanks to the extension made with regard to the amendment called MOCA, in which about forty Italian cities live, through the handicrafts and for the related induced, on the production of artistic ceramics. This is another example of the problems caused by European regulations that do not take into account the peculiarities of our country's productions and typicalities.This is also a clear example, because we are extending something that is wrong, not for the law itself, but because it must be transposed by the member countries according to the needs of local production. Again, many jobs, tourism and the economy of many cities in our country were at risk. I would like to thank once again - I believe it is incredible - that this House is meeting here today, on a Monday in August. I don't know if this has ever happened before... ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u424	GRASSI (M5S). Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the 5 Star Movement will vote in favour of this measure. It is my intention to indicate the spirit that has inspired the majority in the drafting of this standardization act. You will have noticed that it is a particularly small thousandprorogues decree. This is a political choice that already takes into account what we mean by the Government of Change: that it is necessary by an act of primary standardisation to extend the terms is a consequence of the use of primary legislation with an administrative function. It is no coincidence that the first thousand-pronged decree dates back to the early 2000s, that is to say to those years in which the alteration of the functions of the Government and Parliament began progressively, sometimes with a Government replacing Parliament and, in other cases, with a Parliament replacing the Government. We are therefore faced with a measure which in orderly legislation would have no reason to exist: the imposition of time limits is typical administrative activity. We have therefore decided, by reason of the return to these ancient principles, to use this instrument sparingly, with the hope that in the following it will be possible to restore to primary standardization, that is to say to the standardization of Parliament, the characteristics of generality and abstractness typical of the legislation, leaving to the administration the task of indicating the terms. Let me now focus only on some of these measures and provisions, so as not to dread you, using the most significant provisions that give account, precisely, of the spirit that animated us. We have introduced a provision that again allows municipalities to invest in their territories, in order to allow, within four years, to reorganize their activities for urban regeneration and the safety of the suburbs of the metropolitan cities. I also remember the measure that had the task of better coordinating the innovation introduced in 2017 in relation to the plans to reorder the accounts of the municipalities. We know that the reordering plans provide for intermediate checks. In 2017, these reordering plans were amended and the municipalities were allowed to approve longer plans. It has therefore appeared rational to us to establish that intermediate controls, in the light of these new plans, were not valid for the assessment of predictions. Again, I refer to the extension of the deadline for the certification of the implementation of the interventions in order to the extraordinary maintenance programs of the road network of the Provinces and the metropolitan cities, always proof of the great attention for the local realities. Noteworthy is the amendment, apparently of close administrative value, but actually charged with an occult political value, which shifts the deadline to 31 October to allow the committees of national scientific assessments to complete their work. It was necessary to intervene because the law of reference places automatic terms, without being able to have regarding the amount of questions that are submitted from time to time. Here is a typical case in which the law should not carry out the typical activities of the administration. There was a need for primary standardisation to enable certain commissions, burdened with unexpected workloads, to complete the evaluations with due attention and weighting of judgements. I would also remind you of another provision, which is certainly discussed, on the subject of adapting to the fire regulations of school buildings and kindergartens. This is an amendment which has requested our utmost attention. The truth is that the funds that have been allocated have not been allocated within a reasonable period of time to schools and some institutions, despite them, have found themselves in difficulty in carrying out the necessary measures to adapt to the rules in question. We have, therefore, faced a dramatic alternative: not to intervene and determine the closure of these institutions with subsequent inconveniences or introduce a derogation choosing, therefore, the lesser evil. Again, I am thinking of the arrangements for building operations carried out for immediate housing needs following seismic events. Here we have allowed to postpone the deadline for the evaluation of interventions carried out for immediate housing needs. Here too we have had the need to provide for a provision to remedy an emergency. I also remember the intervention that extends the deadline for the amalgamation of the detached sections of the courts for some important islands. The legislation that has imposed the amalgamation, even if it has on the immediate given account of a saving, in the medium period (not even the long period) has evidenced serious criticalities. The difficulties that citizens have encountered in resolving their disputes have a much greater social cost than the savings that have been achieved with that amalgamation; and we know very well what the plight of the judiciary in relation to that initial measure is. We intervened wherever possible and deferred the deadline for the closure of those detached seats. Finally, allow me to comment on the extension of the period for vaccination certificates. Allow me to highlight this passage. It was intended to draw from that amendment a wrong syllogism. [[Comments from the PD Group and Senator Ronzulli.]] Please! The decree-law, moreover, has a high-quality title - this is particularly close to my heart, as I said in this House in another debate - which recalls the dignity of workers and businesses. These are much more prosaically measures on: discipline of the fixed-term contract, administration, redundancy allowances, relocation, ludopathy and tax simplification. A complex measure. Why do I say that the title of this decree-law is not good? Because Parliament, as we know, examines and approves measures aimed at achieving the same social dignity that the Republic must guarantee to citizens and citizens as the beautiful Article 3 of our Constitution states. Therefore, it is not the prerogative of anyone to call a decree-law "dignity," let alone of the Government that, as long as the Italian Constitution is this, it has executive power, so it is not the first-person holder of the legislative power that belongs to Parliament. I come to two questions of merit of the decree-law, the first of which concerns the work being administered. Let us remember that, in its judgment of 11 April 2013, the European Court of Justice ruled that the European Directive on fixed-term work is not applicable to the work being administered, in view of the fact that European legislation on the term contract expressly separates this type of contract from that of the job administered. Another legal confusion in the measure: we have emphasized this in so many interventions, until this morning in the Commission, asking the Government to heal this ambiguous legal framework, which does not correspond to European jurisprudence. I would like to stress another aspect: the raising of the unjustified dismissal allowance of two additional months, as provided for in the decree-law, each year, compared to the provisions of Legislative Decree No 23 of 2015, raises strong doubts of constitutionality, especially in relation to the raising of the threshold of twenty-four monthly, in our opinion for violation of article 3, paragraph 1, of the Constitution insofar as it introduces an unreasonable differentiation between workers and workers hired before March 7, 2015. We all know that the orientation of the jurisprudence has repeatedly reiterated that differences and differentiations of treatment are possible only if they are justified and, in any case, reasonable, for example, to increase employment, which is not the case in this decree-law. In fact, with the same unjustified dismissal on the ground that it is not serious enough to justify dismissal and equal seniority, we will now have a worker or a worker, hired before 7 March 2015, who will receive a lower allowance than the same employee subsequently employed. For us, it is a violation of the constitutional dictation. Then there is the whole question of employment contracts with teachers with a master's degree. Here, too, there is a mess in the provision. The intervention that allows the MIUR one hundred and twenty days of time to give executions to every measure jurisprudential that involves the forfeiture of contracts of employment concluded precisely with teachers in possession of magisterial diploma obtained in the school year 2001-2002, inserted with reservation in the graduations when exhausted, represents a measure that is limited to defer the adoption of a structural solution of the problem involving the pupils, the families and the orderly course of the training the effects of the possible occupational uncertainty of these teachers. Moreover, the abolition of the thirty-six-month rule, without a certain date of competitions, risks causing not only new sanctions for Italy, but the opening of a new season of the precarious. I conclude because the text of the question for a preliminary ruling has already been handed over to the Presidency. The relocation measures appear to be absolutely contrary to the date of conclusion of the investment facilitated and are contrary to the process of attracting investment, which Italy and Italian companies must do. In addition, in this case too, there is a huge increase in litigation - these are standards of dubious applicability - and they are, finally, contrary to the process of internationalization of our companies, which is not reflected in any other reality.

Remember that the limit of extensions does not apply to innovative start-ups for the first four years since their establishment. This is a first step in restoring quality and dignity to work and ensuring security and protection for all citizens. In conclusion, we are going to adopt a law that wants to defeat the fears of Italians and restore their dignity. We want to eliminate the spectre of insecurity, the fear of having no more money, the complexity of state bureaucracy, the fear of having no more work, the anxiety of not being able to choose a future. In our time, and for a long time, the real problem is not the supply of goods and services as such (because we can produce the goods we need a thousand times), but consumption. In order to increase employment and make it stable and secure, we need a policy that can move the levers of domestic demand. These arguments bear witness to the failure of politics, its unconditional surrender and its uselessness. On the other hand, politics has the duty to lay down the conditions for achieving full employment and to guarantee each individual worker as if, in each contract, the negotiating parties are not two (dator and worker), but always three: the two parties preceding the State, which, while in the free market, regulates and regulates relations, having reference to national interests in the fields of production, competition, the dignity of work and participation of workers in the profits and management of the enterprise. For these reasons, we give a firm adherence to the decree of dignity. ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u482	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the decree of dignity is the signal of a government that wants to start a real change, a change that finally begins to put people first, a change that looks to the common good, to the interest of the many and not the few. Increasing this guarantee will ensure that the employer only fires if necessary or if there are really incompatible conditions. As regards the reduction of time limits for time contracts, any employer or undertaking is able to assess within two years whether the employee has the characteristics to be engaged indefinitely. Going beyond these times only favors speculation on a precarious contractual form. In the same way it is elementary common sense to ask for reasons of withdrawal and to introduce a request for reasons justifying the possible need to renew or extend a specific contract. No employer dismisses a capable and reliable employee and, if fired, it is right that you pay properly, because it is not only the company that invests in the employee, but it is also the employee who invests its time and its commitment in the enterprise. Rebalancing a relationship of dignity: this is what the decree-law does. With regard to relocation, the decree-law inserts rules against relocation, provisions that are nothing more than a brake on those who want to take advantage of public funding to speculate. There is no responsible enterprise that, in the case of investment, does not plan with a medium-term strategy of five years, considering in the business plan variable, unexpected and difficulties. Any entrepreneur plans and plans an investment over a period of five years: to relocate first means to have planned the relocation already in the business plan. ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u491	Mr President, representatives of the Government, colleagues and fellow senators, the regulatory provisions that we are called upon to adopt today are necessary in order to preserve the dignity of workers through concrete and effective interventions. Not only that, they respond to the demands of the world of work that have been ignored by those who are currently sitting on the opposition benches and who, with little intellectual honesty, have defined the measure in question as "decree unemployment" and "decree desperation," perhaps to exorcise what they have done in the previous legislature. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] : unemployment and despair, in all social groups, from the employed to the self-employed, and finally with the self-employed. The measure arrives in the Senate after the exhausting vote of the Chamber, confirming the change of step in place compared to what happened in the last legislature, where the measures of the Renzi Government were approved by blows of confidence from the axis, now structured, PD-Forza Italy. The text has also been defined by oppositions as a serious return to the past. This, however, is a note of merit and not of demerit, if we think that it is the same political forces that have repealed Article 18 and that now instrumentally, with an amendment to the decree-law in question, would have wished to reinstate it. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] This is because we want to start a process of overcoming the precariousness! Among the measures against the precarious, I would like to mention in particular the new rules for fixed-term contracts, the maximum duration of which is twenty-four months, with the obligation to indicate the causality, and twelve months without cause, with an additional contribution cost for each renewal that can be there for a maximum of four times. The purpose of this rule is to make it more difficult for companies to adopt the fixed-term contract and to stabilise their employees. It is not true that the new discipline will lead to job losses, as badly supported by the PD. At this time, setting the time limit for the fixed-term contract in twenty-four months instead of thirty-six cannot lead to an increase in unemployment, because after twenty-four months, the company will have an interest in continuing the employment relationship with its worker, in stabilising the employee who will have accumulated sufficient working experience over that period. There is also more. If the time contract exceeds 12 months and the so-called causal ones are not indicated, i.e. the specific assumptions justifying the exceeding of the 12-month limit, the contract shall automatically be transformed without a time limit. This is the real fight against precariousness! [[Applause from the M5S Group]] However, despite the clarity of the rule on the point, the oppositions, being able to do nothing else, completely instrumentally agitate the fear of the future increase of appeals in court, where the causatives are generic. This too, however, is a false reading in the light of the clear scope of the norm. In order to extend the protection of the worker, there is also the increase from one hundred twenty to one hundred and eighty days of the time limit for the appeal of the fixed nature of the employment contract, as is important the provision for a transitional period until 31 October for the entry into force of the rule, which will give companies the time necessary to adapt to the new rules, although, dear Senator of the PD, some companies have already converted to 90% of the fixed-term contracts. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] An example for all is the Fiocchi ammo company Spa of Lecco, a company that was founded in 1876 and to which our applause goes. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] The extension of the rules laid down for the fixed-term employment contract to the administration relationship, the fixing of a quantitative limit according to which the number of workers employed under fixed-term contracts or fixed-term contracts may not exceed 30% of the number of workers engaged without a time limit, the introduction of the case of fraudulent administration, the minimum and maximum limits of the measure of compensation in the event of unlawful dismissal which is changed, but above all the closing rule: the obligation for the Minister of Labour and Social Policy to report annually to Parliament on the employment and financial effects resulting from the application of the rules referred to. We are, of course, faced with a total assumption of responsibility on the part of this Government. There is a contrast to relocation, given the lack of incisiveness of the previous discipline contained in the 2014 law of stability, with the introduction of a financial administrative penalty which, lords of the PD, is by no means a punitive legislation, but a rule that will bring the money of sanctions back into the state budget. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] I should like to conclude, Mr President, by recalling that the decree of dignity is a perfectionable measure, but it is a first step towards the radical change of social and economic policies carried out so far by the PD and Force Italia, the only political forces that still believe they are in the electoral campaign and are demonstrating it these weeks in this House, where there is instead a strong and cohesive majority of Government. [[Applause from the M5S Group. Congratulations.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u501	Mr President, since 1997 we have witnessed a progressive precariousness of the world of work, with the motivation that those so-called reforms would modernise the regulation of access to employment, with the consequent increase in employment. They told us that those who opposed the so-called reforms were out of time, represented the old and that the market would solve everything, bringing demand closer to supply. After the so-called Treu package, rules have been followed on standards that have accepted all the requests for flexibility of the employers, but nothing or little has been done on support for so-called flexible workers. In this way the work has been systematically precarious, returning almost to its nineteenth-century conception, re-introduced in fact the piecework, calling it with a nice English name: gig economy. Wages have decreased, and domestic consumption has collapsed causing instability and uncertainty, which have resulted in a worsening of the recession and delayed exit from the crisis that exploded in 2008. All this has stolen the future and hope of an entire generation. Our young people have been forced to work ever shorter, less paid, less protected and with little hope of stabilising the relationship: all factors which, for example, have prevented their exit from their families of origin. And someone, after causing these epochal disasters, also had the courage to call them "girls." Those who have not accepted the development of skills acquired and compromises to the bottom, and have had the opportunity, have chosen to leave the country, if of choice can speak to each other. The disaster carried out by previous governments not only destroyed the lives of young people, but also involved 40-year-olds and 50-year-olds, who had lost their jobs because of the crisis and were unable to find a new one: fathers and mothers of families, workers, small entrepreneurs, left alone without systematic support from the State that would allow them to maintain their families. We have had to witness with sorrow a succession of tragic suicides of citizens like us, decent people overwhelmed by despair. The mantra of these years has been flexibility. But those who ruled forgot to support it with the necessary safeguards for those who were the victim of this flexibility in terms of reducing wages. We want to protect all of this, and this majority wants to do so. Thus, the decree-law under consideration widens the audience of young people who can benefit from the exemption to promote employment. Additional contributions on time contracts are provided for in order to facilitate permanent processing, providing for employers to be able to recover such contributions in the event of stabilisation. Vouchers are re-introduced only and exclusively in the sectors where they are needed, that is, where they are indispensable to bring out pockets of black work, but at the same time preventing the abuses of the past that had caused their cancellation. At last there are constraints to prevent companies that benefit from public contributions aimed at safeguarding the employment levels of relocation after having pocketed the contributions. The requirement for a split payment is eliminated for self-employed workers, which has put them in great difficulty. It will finally be possible to offset the debts of debit cards and claims on public administrations. The ban on gambling advertising has been imposed since January 1, 2019, and we know how much this measure will benefit families, since gambling has thrown even more into despair those people who, in economic difficulty due to the loss of work, have believed in the false myth of easy winning. With this act we begin to solve the problems of the world of work, laying the foundations for starting a virtuous path that leads to the increase of employment and an improvement of the supply of labour in terms of stability and durability of the same. The Dignity Decree finally builds a master wall of rights; a wall that will stop the negative spiral of the degradation of rights and help to reverse the course by restoring dignity to workers and citizens all. Those who go to the workplace must be aware that they have duties, but also rights. Today we restore hope, we restore dignity. [[Applause from the L-SP-PSd'Az and M5S Groups]]

Well, instant lotteries are the first forms of gambling practiced by minors: three out of four of them report that they had had no obstacle to daring. Then you know that, by law, today the "Gratta and Vinci" must declare the odds of winning. Well, a coupon that costs 5 euros and makes me win 5 euros is counted among the winnings. But, if I played 5 and won 5, it's not a win and it's not even a refund, because in 95 percent of cases those people retake immediately and go into the mouth to the probability of losing. This is for people treated by chickens and such shame ends today. Then, to venture on the slot machines and videolottery you will need to enter the tax card. It is a measure that industry is already ready to accept. Well, in the future it will be possible to provide a dedicated card, with time and expense limits, in relation to income, and to extend controls similar to all forms of gambling. The term "ludopathy" disappears. Why does the gambling industry like the term "ludopathy"? It's like saying you're sick for a game: the problem is you, the fault is yours, you've done something wrong, you've gone too far. No, today we know that it is an addiction and as such we define it; it is an addiction that also derives from the environment in which people live and from the characteristics of the aggressive forms of gambling that are proposed. We will have other possibilities in the future, which today have not entered into the decree-law under consideration: to extend access to the anti-usury fund to families; to exclude places of chance near churches, schools and sensitive places; to preserve from chance the hours of lunch and dinner in the family. And we will have to and we will be able to do this by guaranteeing minimum protection for citizens in all the regions of Italy, without prejudice to the legislative power of the Regions and the regulations of the Municipalities to determine even greater forms of protection. We know that in the world of university, in the world of research, European funding programmes are all three years old. Researchers throughout Europe are engaged in three-year term contracts. Proceeding in this way does not fight precariousness, but favours a downward game towards the black work that is still a scourge too present. Other companies, on the other hand, will focus on external advice, pushing young workers to open the VAT number. This road will also be crossed with those who are currently unable to access preferential schemes, if the taxation of the flat-rate scheme is extended to ordinary VAT consignments. The most serious consequence will be that the worker will live outside the context of work, limiting himself to providing the benefit remotely and not attending the company day after day And those with a minimum of business experience know how important it is for the stabilization of a boy's job to know him, to appreciate day after day his character compatibility with his colleagues and the fact that he feels and becomes part of a team. The company is made by the people and the decisions of an employer are made not only by a strict analysis of the costs and economic benefits. It is serious that this Government, in which there are forces that have an important root in the productive sectors, does not understand this. We have data from the INPS which, as you have pointed out more than anyone, are all too conservative forecasts. We must reduce the cost of work indefinitely and it is certainly a good thing that something has already been done in the House. Above all, however, we must put companies in a position to compete and innovate. We therefore share the concerns expressed by companies in recent weeks and that the work done in the House has failed to escape in any way. In fact, it may have aggravated it, if I think about automatism after twelve months from the term contract to the permanent one. On relocations I share the principle, but the rule, as it is written, opens up scenarios of legal uncertainty. It could have been improved. We were hoping that the House would do so and that this House would be given the same chance. It has been decided otherwise and we must take note of it. That is why my judgment on the measure is negative and I am now announcing my vote against, because the decree-law under consideration, Minister, certainly does not create a job, but I am sure and sure that it will destroy jobs and that does not serve the country. ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u557	The general debate is closed. I call the representative of the Government.
ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u558	Mr President, kind senators and kind senators, first of all I would like to take this opportunity to respond to all or almost all of the comments that have been made. I have not been able to listen to the first half hour of debate and I apologise, but I will at least try to explain to the Assembly of the Senate of the Republic what was the reason that led us to carry out such a measure and to do so because of necessity and urgency. The first point, which was also raised in the debate in the Chamber of Deputies last week, was precisely because of the reason for adopting a decree-law. The decree-law according to us is right in that it intervenes on four emergencies of our society, that is to say the precarious, the emergency of the relocations (in the first two months of this Government we have had three cases of companies that from morning to evening have sent hundreds of letters of dismissal to their employees and have gone abroad); then we have an emergency that we have to face with all our strength and it is that of the gambling, the gambling, and that of the bureaucracy. I would like to make two premises, the first of which is that this is not an instrument that solves all the problems in this sector, but it is beginning to deal with them in contrast with the past. I say that using this term, but it is also natural that it is so. We have been political forces that have opposed each other in the past years because we had two different recipes for the problems of the country; Today these political forces have alternated, some have gone to the government and others are in opposition. I also consider it normal that there are different ideas and everyone takes responsibility for the idea of a country that carries on. I was intrigued, both in the debate in the Chamber and this afternoon in the Senate, by the fact that the extremes of this Chamber accuse the decree-law in question of being either too punitive or of having done too little for the precarious. It's no accident. We decided to take a step-by-step approach to the first issue of this measure, namely the precariousness, first of all acting on one point, putting us in line with the duration of fixed-term contracts in the European average, which is twenty-four months; we did so by re-introduced the causatives from the second contract and by increasing the sanctions for unfair dismissals. For someone it is too little; for others it is too much. I say that this is a first common sense intervention and of course we take full responsibility for the measures we have. Compared to those who say that the litigation will increase, I answer that the litigation is zero because its object, which was one of the rights of the worker to be able to be hired on a fixed time for specific reasons, has been eliminated, because the normality for us is the contract to indefinite time, while the fixed-term contract must serve for specific situations of the company, but not from the first. It is logical that in the first employment relationship between the employer and the employee you should know, you should have the opportunity and the opportunity to appreciate the skills and also the defects of the worker. On the precarious side, I would also humbly like to give this House the time horizon with which this measure operates, which I believe will allow companies and companies to metabolize the change in regulations. This is because for the existing contracts there is a transitional rule that acts from 31 October, and in any case, for the new contracts the first twelve months are without causality. Someone will ask what happens after the twenty-four months and I answer by asking what happened before after the thirty-six months. Is there a divine law whereby thirty - six months is spent indefinitely and twenty - four months is fired? I don't think so. I simply believe that by doing so we will have the new contracts that allow a relationship of knowledge between employer and employee in the first twelve months without cause and, for contracts in place from 31 October, the new legislation will begin to explain the effects in the legal order. Why October 31st? For a simple reason: because in the budget law we want to give more advantages to entrepreneurs on the contract indefinitely, lowering the cost of labour and making that the normal contract, the normal; therefore we allow them to get there. [[Applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups]] Then it is clear that there are many aspects that lead us to address the issue of fixed-term contracts and to restrict their use. I think that there are many young people, many young people in this country who are living in the early years of their age a relationship with work in my opinion devastating from a cultural point of view. A culture of work is created, in the new generations, which can be devastating, that is, based on the idea of working without earning, in order to say that it is working. It is an increasingly developed trend, involving so many people who, in order to say that they are working, are willing to lose money, to give up a salary. This is happening together with another phenomenon, which is the alternation of administration contracts, determined contracts, administration, then there is stop and go. All this is creating more precariousness and creating a culture of work that is devastating, because those boys who at the age of eighteen begin this process that never ends will begin to think that that alone is work and will abandon any ambition of personal and family stability. I do not believe that with this decree-law we will solve all these problems, but at least we have started to limit them, because we are convinced that more stable work gives more productivity to our companies. And the topic of the productivity of our companies is one of the biggest problems we have from the point of view of the economic development of Italy. Added to this are consumption: those who have a more stable contract have more opportunities to plan their lives, consume more and therefore, once again, benefit companies. Then we may not agree, but I am not surprised because - I say this without irony and without controversy - other recipes have been followed in previous years, which other European countries are still following, I recognize. Then, what is the consensus of those European countries that are carrying out those overprecarious reforms, we will see it. We had a different mandate, which we included in the government contract and which is the contrast of the precarious. We will, of course, confront each other in this House, but I have to say that the recipes on this are very discordant. We don't have a common vision. Because when it is said that we have to increase flexibility, which is a kind expression to say very often that we have to increase precariousness to that extent, that we have to increase the number of contracts, not of employees, sometimes of contracts to a few days or a few weeks, then it is a problem. The subject of small and medium-sized Italian entrepreneurs has been mentioned a lot, but with these rules we give a little contractual weight, instead, to many young and old people who work in shopping centres, in multinationals; sometimes their union representatives do not even matter; let alone when they ask for a right in the workplace. And we have been through it - I imagine - many in this House with their children, their grandchildren or themselves. Of course, we will judge the effect of these rules. We assume responsibility for this as a government, but I believe it is time that we no longer pursue the god market, and begin to put the person and his needs at the centre again. [[Applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups and Senator Buccarella]] In fact, already in these first two months of government, I happened to have to deal with the majority of Italian companies, which have always been very cooperative when there are crises, and then I happened to have to deal with very large companies, very often foreign, that on the national territory were good with the budgets, but they were canceling much of their production. Well, there are two kinds of companies: the one that says that it wants to work with the Government to find a solution to its employees, even if it cannot keep them, and the one that is also good with its business plans, but that tells us: either you make me exploit them or we leave Italy. A government can't possibly be blackmailed like that. When they tell me this, the answer is one: to protect Italian citizens and not give in to the idea of making them exploit them. [[Applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups. Comments from the PD Group]] There's a lot to do about the bureaucracy. I do not deceive myself that by disabling the meter, the meter and the split payment for the professionals we have solved the problems; for Christ's sake. We have so much more to do and we are working on this, also with undersecretaries like Dario Galli, who helps me to MISE by working on the dossier on simplification for businesses. We will work on the budget law, but also before that. However, we can say, as has been said before by someone, that disabling the meter income, predicting a last communication for the meter and then making it exceed with e-invoicing, eliminating the split payment for professionals, so as to give them more liquidity is definitely a countertrend signal compared to the past, when using professionals and entrepreneurs like cows to milk to find the covers to the budget law. [[Applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups]] We had a wide-ranging debate on relocations in the House of Deputies. It is said that this rule also affects internationalizations. First of all, I have to say that very often some clever - I am not talking about the parliamentary debate of course - in the general debate, in the mainstream, deliberately confuses internationalization with relocation to justify behaviors that are not justifiable by some companies, which go away from today to tomorrow. There are cases that required a law ten years ago and probably wouldn't even exist today. There are cases of companies that received funds from the state (and we all went to those gates) and then left; some came four months ago to MISE to say that their budgets were fine, that they were ready to hire new people and then, four months later, they sent a letter of dismissal to everyone. Some will say that the rule on relocations already existed in some ministerial regulations of the Ministry of Economic Development for some funds. It is true, but we, with this rule, are saying something else: that every kind of fund that enters the coffers of a company demands from that company seriousness and respect for the State. [[Applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups]] This means - and you will see it from the norm - that, then, each Ministry will be called to create its own regulations to recover these resources. We sent it back to the administrations precisely because we know that there is not only one Ministry that provides finance to companies, but there are many. There are funding in various ways and the general principle is that if you want a helping hand in developing the economy of your company, on the national territory, hiring Italian citizens, we will stretch out red carpets and give you a helping hand. But if you want to leave, after a few years, teasing the state, you have to give us back the money, interest and you have to pay a fine equal to four times the money you took. [[Applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups]] We have made a differentiation: in the case of non-EU we also expect a sanction; within the European Union we take back money with interest. So we have also identified a standard for relocations within the European Union. Then it will be said that this is how internationalization is hit. On this I am very quiet for a simple reason: the funds for internationalization are another thing and, among other things, it will always be the Ministry to move the action of recovery of the money that we have given to the enterprises. So, in any case, it's at the request of the Ministry. Why did we make such a rule? Because very often these companies show up at crisis tables and say: "No, we're leaving, we don't want to listen." Sometimes they don't even show up and the state has no contractual means to convince these companies to stay. In fact, it is they who say: "Thank you that I relocate only one plant, otherwise I ruin it." No, this time we'll ruin you a little, if you'll excuse me, because we have some rules to get the money back. [[Applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups and Senator Buccarella]] President, I come to the conclusion: on the subject of gambling in the House of Deputies (of course there are different visions depending on the groups and perhaps for the opposition groups it was not a satisfactory debate), as already acknowledged by some senator who spoke, there have been many steps forward, also thanks to the parliamentary debate. We have intervened with a rule that prohibits any kind of gambling advertising, online, television or other forms of sponsorship. Then we had identified a 5% sanction, but thanks to an opposition amendment we have reached 20% sanction. Thanks to Ms Carnivali, we have inserted the message - which is like what we find on cigarette packs - which is seriously damaging to health on all gambling products. Thanks to an opposition amendment in the Commission, we have identified an instrument, that of the tax code, to use slot machines, which also makes it impossible for children to use them. This is not enough. There's so much more to do. We started doing this because a study by Caritas tells us that 90 percent of the people who gamble in Italy get to know gambling through TV commercials and online. They'll be banned. Some say that in the rest of Europe they are not banned and that, therefore, in the rest of the European countries it will happen. For once we have a positive record in Europe: we are the first country in Europe to have banned gambling commercials. [[Applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups]] I would like to say one more thing about the PREU: the more we increase the PREU and the lower the gambling in Italy, because it is a deterrent. By increasing taxation on a sector, you penalise it. So we will also use PREU as a deterrent to the proliferation of gambling and continue to do so in the coming years. With one awareness, but of course in the general debate another conviction can be gained: we are aware that some of these games must remain in the Italian order to prevent their absence from producing an incentive to illegal gambling. We will also fight the illegal game: there are already very interesting proposals that have come from the Customs and Monopolies Agency and this will also allow us to continue a fight that has been waged and for which I want to thank our law enforcement forces. I am aware of one thing and on this we should then make a great reflection: sometimes the illegal game and the legal game merge. Very often we have met with our law enforcement forces who have seized legal slot rooms in the hands of criminal organizations. We should therefore make a 360 degree reflection on how much this kind of game can continue to exist in our system. Of course, much more could be done. I have listened to many speeches on aspects that are not dealt with in this decree-law. I have heard from many senators advice to insert other norms in this decree-law, but we must agree: there have been preconstitutional preconstitutionals, to the House for example, for the fact that this decree-law was an omnibus provision; if the same people who presented the constitutional preconstitutionals then come to say that we have to add more and make it even more omnibus creates a bit of a short circuit. [[Applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups and the government benches]] The only thing I can say is that with this decree-law we decided to attack emergencies. We will still have to intervene in these areas. We have now done so with the conviction that we must start to reverse the trend in all these areas, where possible working together. There is, however, a speech that I also gave to the Members of Parliament, speaking to the House last week. It is a right enshrined in the Rules of Procedure that of the opposition to overturn this measure, because it does not feel the same way as we do (right also used by the parliamentarians of the opposition during the examination in the Chamber of Deputies). However, it is a majority right to bring the measure home, because we are convinced of the measures we have introduced within the decree-law. Finally, the measure contains measures on two other topics: school and amateur sports associations. We'll have a chance to discuss this. On this I say only one thing: there is a sentence that has hit a category of teachers. We take the burden and responsibility of making a decision and start intervening to save the work of these people. [[Extended applause from the M5S and L-SP-PSd'Az groups and the government benches]] . MALPEZZI (PD). You fired them!

ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u610	Mr President, I am very pleased to interrupt this flow of votes - we are going very quickly - and I am pleased not only because we are taking some oxygen in this way, but also because this is an opportunity for me to speak with the Government and with the majority (I say this to some regret), after not having been able to do so during the two days we worked in the Commission on this measure. In fact, Mr President, the work has been a sort of solipsistic sitting on the part of the opposition, who have rightly done an amending job, but have not received a single response and a single intercourse by the majority and the government. As a witness to the action of some of the protagonists of this majority in the last legislature, the regret is that they have recorded a sudden transition from a bold opposition, with even revolutionary traits, to an institutional aplomb, to the limits of the aphony, which is an element of regret, but also of frustration. In fact, I think that we should grow collectively in the intercourse between majority and opposition. If I may, Mr President, also seeking your attention and the attention of the House, because we are talking about a subject, at least from my point of view, which is very important, what we are presenting in this article, among others, is an amendment that passes as a proposal for the re-introduction of Article 18, but - if I am allowed a small element - the subject is much greater than its content. The theme is if we want to declare closed forever, in a way even cross-cutting to the political forces, the season in which in this country it has been thought - my Parent in general discussion has said very well - that a social and productive economic system could only be raised as long as it tampered with the rules of the labour market, which in the end, at every successful attempt, meant to compress the sphere of workers' rights. It happened in stages and in alternate political cycles: right-wing governments did so and they did so - alas - even governments that declared themselves left-wing or centre-left. In the last parliamentary term, the last trade mark of the workers' statute was removed incomprehensible. Look, Mr President, I say this to you and through you to the members of the majority and at this point also to a good piece of the opposition members: Article 18, which provides for reinstatement in the workplace in the event of unlawful dismissal, in the event that precisely the worker proves the wrong cause and therefore through the decision of a judge decide not for compensation but to be integrated in the workplace, is not a fetish of the past, but it is exactly the embankment - I would say even of civilizations - that many workers, millions of workers and workers have used in this country to defend their rights. And I say this not because I believe that Article 18 is the instrument to raise the fate of a country, but because - as some say - that article and that instrument is useless, we must not touch it, because it concerns the sphere of workers' rights. We think it should be an act of reasoning about the need to return to that instrument. I say this for the past, of course, and I have already told those who have misguidedly deleted this instrument. But I also say this to the representatives of the majority: here I see Minister Di Maio who in the past became the author of this battle and who at the first great useful opportunity was not in condition, I do not know whether political, personal or in the relationship between his piece of majority and the League, to carry on this just battle. I do not allow myself to make a pathetic appeal to the Government, which is often made when the amendments are tabled, to ask him to reconsider, because he will not, but humbly ask the Government in the future to seriously reflect on the need to re-introduce an element that is not only civilization of the law, but civilization of a country. [[Applause from the Mixed Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u619	Parent (PD). Mr President, with Amendment 2.15 we seek to remedy the legal ambiguity that exists throughout Article 2. As some of my colleagues have already mentioned during the general discussion, there are two different rules for the term contract and the contract being administered. They refer to two different European directives, linked in turn to different judgments of the Court of Justice of the European Union. The measure is confusing between the two institutions. With this amendment we try to heal this confusion, also because the widespread propaganda on these issues, which does not correspond to the truth, concerns the fact that the contract being administered is not a legal privilege, but a different contractual institution, consisting of the relationship between the agency being administered, the worker and the user company. I would remind this House that when the worker goes on a mission to the user company, he is subject to all the employment contracts that are in that company, starting from the economic parity and everything provided for in the contract. Comparing this also in causality is wrong legally, and it is wrong in the reality of this institute of administration. Article 2 is the example of the confusion that is being made and does not correspond to the truth. [[Applause from the PD Group.]] It corresponds to some kind of propaganda, which I heard here from Senator Paragon. These are matters that do not correspond to the truth. You are in breach of a principle of reality with respect to work and, since work is the life of people, this is very dangerous. [[Applause from the PD Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2018-08-06-LEG18-Senato-sed-32.ana.u625	Mr President, I shall be brief, as I have already spoken. We call for the abolition of the rule which re-introduces - by extending them very much - the vouchers for tourism and agriculture. We ask for the repeal tout court: for tourism the possibility of use is extended even under the five employees; for agriculture, and in general - this is very serious - the possibility of time use is given from three to ten days. This rule, in fact - and I also say this to the Minister - puts very much at risk the law n. 199 of 2016 on the caporalate, especially in the part where the use is extended to ten days and can spread the four hours over the same ten days. I therefore ask you to think very carefully about this issue, because, among other things, in agriculture - I repeat - you cannot understand the need for this rule, especially since the national contract of farmers also provides for the possibility of hiring for a working day. It is in this way to take away from women the guarantee of motherhood through the use of vouchers, which risks being fraudulent and a kind of safe conduct for black work. That is why we are calling for a repeal.
